Regular Session, 2004
HOUSE BILL NO. 2
BY REPRESENTATIVES HAMMETT, ALARIO, AND THOMPSON AND SENATORS
MOUNT AND HEITMEIER
AN ACT
1
To provide with respect to the capital outlay budget and the capital outlay program for state
2
government, state institutions, and other public entities; to provide for the
3
designation of projects and improvements; to provide for the financing thereof
4
making appropriations from certain sources; and to provide for related matters.
5
Be it enacted by the Legislature of Louisiana:
6
Section 1.  The following sums or so much thereof as may be necessary are hereby
7
appropriated out of any monies in the state treasury from the sources specified, from federal
8
funds and self-generated revenues belonging to the state of Louisiana and/or collected by
9
boards, commissions, departments, and agencies thereof, all for making capital outlay and
10
for the purposes and in the amounts specified herein for the Fiscal Year commencing July
11
1, 2004, and ending June 30, 2005, subject to the conditions thereafter provided:
12
A project with funding payable from General Obligation Bonds Priority 1 indicates
13
that the funding for  the project includes the reauthorization of funding which was previously
14
on a line of credit granted by the State Bond Commission in Fiscal Year 2003-2004.
15
As used in this Section, the term "general obligation bonds" means state general
16
obligation bonds or other evidences of indebtedness payable from the Bond Security and
17
Redemption Fund.
18
The amount set forth in this Section opposite each project, or so much thereof as may
19
be necessary, is hereby appropriated and payable from the proceeds available from the sale
20
of general obligation bonds, unless a different source of funding is indicated.  Where such
21
a different source of funding is indicated, the corresponding amount set forth, or so much
22
thereof as may be necessary, is hereby appropriated and payable from the source of funding
23
indicated; provided, however, amounts appropriated and payable from federal funds may be
24
encumbered or expended only to the extent such amounts shall have been committed or
25


ENROLLED
H.B. NO. 2
Page 2 of 177
received and amounts for any project payable fro m revenue bonds in excess of ten million
1
dollars may be encumbered or expended only after approval by the Joint Legislative
2
Committee on Capital Outlay.  Pursuant to the provisions of R.S. 39:82, funds for planning,
3
acquisition, construction, and major repair projects appropriated by this act may be retained
4
until completion of the project, even if no bona fide liability exists on the last day of the
5
fiscal year.
6
This Act shall constitute the comprehensive state capital outlay budget for the 2004-
7
2005 Fiscal Year.  Projects set forth which are payable from the proceeds available from the
8
sale of general obligation bonds shall have the priorities as indicated.  Requests for lines of
9
credit must be filed with the Commissioner of Administration.  No general obligation bonds
10
shall be sold, nor cash lines of credit granted to fund any lower priority project prior to the
11
sale of general obligation bonds or the granting of cash lines of credit to fund all higher
12
priority projects (the "general rule"), except as hereinafter provided.  This prohibition against
13
funding a lower priority project shall not apply upon a showing of an impossibility or
14
impracticality either to proceed with all unfunded higher priority projects or to proceed with
15
the funding of such projects or any part thereof through the issuance of general obligation
16
bonds for any reason.  Such showing shall be stated in reasonable detail in a certificate or
17
certificates signed by the state agency responsible for administering the funding, and filed
18
with the Commissioner of Administration and the State Bond Commission (hereinafter
19
referred to as "the commission").  Upon a determination by the commission that proceeding
20
with such a project or the funding thereof through the issuance of general obligation bonds
21
is impractical or impossible, the commission  may then proceed with the sale of general
22
obligation bonds or the granting of cash lines of credit without regard to the priority of the
23
unfunded projects determined to be impossible or impractical.
24
Upon recommendation by the appropriate person or agency, if it is determined at any
25
time that proceeding with the project or with the funding thereof is no longer impossible or
26
impractical, the State Bond Commission may rescind the certificate of impossibility or
27
impracticality and may proceed with the project or with the issuance of general obligation
28
bonds or the granting of cash lines of credit to fund the project.
29
Alternatively, the Interim Emergency Board (hereinafter referred to as "the board"),
30
upon reviewing certificates as described above and determining that proceeding with such
31


ENROLLED
H.B. NO. 2
Page 3 of 177
project or the funding thereof through the issuance of general obligation bonds is impossible
1
or impractical, may designate a lower priority for such project.  The board's determination
2
and lower priority designation shall be submitted to the members of the Legislature for their
3
approval in accordance with the provisions of Chapter 3-B of Subtitle I of Title 39 of the
4
Louisiana Revised Statutes of 1950.  In accordance with the provisions of Chapter 3-B of
5
Subtitle I of Title 39 of the Louisiana Revised Statutes of 1950, whenever at the written
6
request of the appropriate legislators and agency, the board determines that an adjustment
7
to a project description originally set forth in such capital budget adopted by the Legislature
8
for a current fiscal year is necessary to correct a mistake in a project description, clarify a
9
description, change the scope of a description, or make any other adjustment the board
10
deems necessary to implement the purpose of the project, such adjustment shall only become
11
effective upon the approval by majority vote of the elected members of each house of the
12
Legislature in the manner provided for in Chapter 3-B of Subtitle I of Title 39 of the
13
Louisiana Revised Statutes of 1950.
14
If such approval is granted, notification by the chairman or secretary of  the board
15
of the results of the balloting shall be transmitted to the State Bond Commission, which may
16
then proceed with the sale of general obligation bonds or the granting of cash lines of credit
17
for each change approved in accordance with the general rule.
18
With respect to Priorities 1 through 5, a lower priority project may be moved to a
19
higher priority only upon a showing of an emergency or other cause not reasonably
20
anticipated by the Legislature, which shall be stated in reasonable detail in a certificate
21
signed by the head of the appropriate department, political subdivision, or agency and filed
22
with the Commissioner of Administration and the board.  Upon a determination by the board
23
that such project should be moved to a higher priority because of an emergency or other
24
cause not reasonably anticipated by the Legislature, the board shall designate a higher
25
priority to the project.  The matter then shall be submitted to and be subject to the same
26
appro val by the members of the Legislature in the manner previously indicated in this
27
Section.  If such approval is granted, notification by the chairman or secretary of the board
28
of the change of priority shall be transmitted to the State Bond Commission, which then may
29
proceed with the sale of general obligation bonds or the granting of cash lines of credit in
30
accordance with the general rule.  As to projects within a priority, the State Bond
31


ENROLLED
H.B. NO. 2
Page 4 of 177
Commission shall determine when such project, or phases thereof, shall be funded.  In
1
making such determination, the State Bond Commission shall consider the amount of
2
proceeds of general obligation bonds to be made available in light of money and capital
3
market conditions; the expenditure requirements of the project; the time required for
4
architectural plans and drawings, public bidding, and site acquisition and preparation;
5
availability of receipt of federal monies; litigation affecting the undertaking or completion
6
of a project, federal tax laws and regulations regarding the sale of municipal bonds; and
7
other items of a similar nature bearing upon the possibility or the necessity to commence a
8
project or any particular phase thereof. 
9
Any other provision of this Act to the contrary notwithstanding, projects described
10
in Priority 5 of this Act shall not be moved to a higher priority except upon one of the
11
following conditions:
12
(1)  Funding is necessary to complete or additionally fund a project located elsewhere
13
in the capital outlay act.  Determination of such condition shall be made by the head of the
14
appropriate department or agency by means of a certificate to that effect filed with the
15
Interim Emergency Board, who shall, by mail ballot, request approval of such change and
16
if approved by a majority of each house of the Legislature it shall be funded, or
17
(2)  Unless required by an emergency or other cause not reasonably anticipated by
18
the Legislature, to be in accordance with procedures set forth in this Section.  However, the
19
State Bond Commission may authorize a noncash line of credit for any project in Priority
20
5 which represents that portion of construction contracts which were let during a previous
21
fiscal year, or equipment or construction contracts which will be let during the current fiscal
22
year, but which will not require cash expenditures during the year.  Such noncash lines of
23
credit, when combined with previously sold bonds or with higher priority bonds or lines of
24
credit, or with funds appropriated from other sources, will provide sufficient appropriated
25
funds to award equipment or construction contracts.
26
Upon approval by the State Bond Commission, and subject to compliance with its
27
policies and procedures and the procedures set forth herein, a line of credit providing for the
28
use of funds in anticipation of the sale of general obligation bonds may be granted to the
29
appropriate agency or department from the Comprehensive Capital Outlay Escrow Account,
30
or if there is not a sufficient amount available in such account, from other available state
31


ENROLLED
H.B. NO. 2
Page 5 of 177
cash in the state treasury, for capital improvement projects for which bond proceeds are
1
appropriated by a current capital budget, prior to the actual issuance of bonds for such
2
projects, unless the attorney general advises that an impediment exists to the valid issuance
3
of the bonds on the date such line of credit is granted. 
4
All bonds that are to be repaid, in whole or in part, by revenues generated by the
5
project shall be designated as "Reimbursement Bonds".  The State Bond Commission shall
6
enter into such agreements as are necessary to provide that the agency or subdivision, whose
7
project is reimbursable, shall pay the bond debt costs into the Bond Security and Redemption
8
Fund.  Such costs may be paid annually or at the end of the term of the bonds issued as
9
determined by the State Bond Commission.
10
Priority 1 general obligation bond funding shall be limited to:
11
(1)  Projects and amounts which were previously authorized by Act 24 of the 2003
12
Regular Session of the Legislature; and which were granted cash lines of credit by the State
13
Bond Commission.  The bonds to fund these projects were not sold in Fiscal Year 2003-
14
2004.
15
(2)  The reauthorization of the portion of the outstanding Fiscal Year 2003-2004
16
Priority 5 Non-Cash Lines of Credit for projects which will require cash expenditures in
17
Fiscal Year 2004-2005 and therefore must be converted to Cash Lines of Credit in Fiscal
18
Year 2004-2005.
19
(3)  Commitments made in the Higher Education Desegregation Settlement
20
Agreement.
21
Priority 2 general obligation bond projects are for the completion of plans and
22
specifications, land acquisition, site preparation, or for construction which will begin by the
23
first or second quarter of the fiscal year.  It is presently anticipated that the listed projects
24
will require the sale of bonds or extension of lines of credit within six months after adoption
25
of this Act.
26
Priority 3 general obligation bond projects are for the preparation of plans and
27
specifications, land acquisition, site preparation, or for construction which will begin in the
28
third quarter of the fiscal year.  It is presently anticipated that the listed projects will require
29
the sale of bonds or extension of lines of credit during the current fiscal year.
30

Priority 4 general obligation bond projects are for the preparation of plans and
1
specifications, land acquisition, site preparation, or for construction.  It is presently
2
anticipated that the listed projects will require the sale of bonds or extension of lines of
3
credit during the fiscal year if all higher priority projects are funded or declared impossible
4
or impractical.
5
Priority 5 general obligation bond funding shall be limited to funding for the portion
6
of construction contracts or equipment purchases which will be bid and awarded during the
7
fiscal year, but which will not require cash expenditures during the fiscal year.  These funds,
8
when combined with previously sold bonds or with higher priority bonds on lines of credit,
9
or with funds appropriated from other sources, will provide sufficient appropriated funds to
10
award contracts.  Non-Cash lines of credit shall be required prior to the award of these
11
contracts.
